President.
[F. R. Doc. 48-3446; Filed, Apr. 19, 1948;  
8:48 a. m.]
TITLE 11— -ATOMIC ENERGY
Chapter I— United States Atomic 
Energy ‘Commission  
[Circular No. 1]
P art 60—D omestic  U ranium  P rogram
TEN YEAR G U A RA N TEED  MINIMUM PRICE
§  60.1 Ten year guaranteed minimum  
price — (a) Guarantee.  To stimulate do­
mestic5  production of uranium and in the 
interest of the -common defense and se­
curity the United States Atomic Energy 
Commission hereby establishes the guar­
anteed minimum prices specified in par­
agraph (b) of this section, for the de­
livery to the Commission, in accordance 
with the terms of this section during the 
ten calendar years following its effective 
date, of domestic refined uranium, high- 
grade uranium-bearing ores- and me­
chanical concentrates, in not less than 
the quantity and grade specified in par­
agraph (e) of this section. This guaran­
tee does not apply to uranium-bearing 
ores of the Colorado Plateau area, com­
monly known as carnotite-type or ros- 
coelite-type ores, prices for which are 
established by § 60.3.
Note : The term “domestic” in this section,  
referring to uranium, uranium-bearing ores  
and mechanical concentrates, means such  
uranium, ores, and concentrates produced  
from deposits within the United States, its  
territories, possessions and the Canal Zone.
(b) Guaranteed minimum prices.  The 
following minimum prices are estab­
lished:
(1) For uranium-bearing ores and 
mechanical concentrates, $3.50 per 
pound of U3 O s (uranium oxide) deter­
mined by the Commission to be recover­
able, less cost per pound of refining such 
ores or concentrates to standards of 
purity required for the Commission’s 
operations, to be determined by the Com­
mission after assay of a representative 
sample.
(2) For refined uranium products, 
$3.50 per pound contained U3 0 8  (uranium 
oxide).
Prices are f. o. b. railroad cars or trucks 
at shipping point designated by the 
Commission convenient to mine, mill, or 
refinery. Weights are avoirdupois dry 
weight.
(c) MaJcirlg an offer.  Anyone who has 
domestic refined uranium, high-grade 
uranium-bearing ores, or mechanical 
concentrates of the quantity and grade 
specified in paragraph (e) of this sec­
tion, may offer it for delivery to the 
Commission by sending a letter or tele­
gram addressed as follows:
United States Atomic Energy Commission,
Post Office Box 30, Ansonia Station,
New York 23, N. Y.
A ttention: Division of Raw Materials.  
With each offer there should be- fur­
nished a representative ten-pound sam­
ple and the following information:
(1) Location of property;
(2) Character of material offered for 
delivery (state whether refined uranium, 
mechanical concentrates, or uranium­
bearing ores, indicating approximate 
composition);
(3) Amount of material offered;
(4) Location of material offered;
(5) Origin of material If offered by 
other than producer;
(6) If material is owned, in whole or
in part, by any person other than tne 
person making the offer, the name of 
each person having such ownership and 
nature of his rights; and
(7) Name and address of person mak­
ing the offer.
Note : The reporting requirements hereof  
have been approved by the Bureau of the  
Budget pursuant to the Federal Reports Act  
of 1942.
(d) Purchase contract . Upon receipt 
of an offer and sample, aii analysis of 
the sample will be made. If the sample 
and the information furnished are de­
termined by the Commission to meet the 
conditions of this section, the Commis­
sion will forward to the person making 
the offer a form of contract containing 
applicable terms and conditions ready 
for his acceptance. Prices will be not 
less than the applicable prices of para­
graph (b) of this section.
(e) Minimum quantity and grade.  No 
delivery will be accepted under this sec­
tion of less than ten short tons (2,000 
pounds per ton) of ores or mechanical 
concentrates, nor of ore or mechanical 
concentrates which assay less than 10 
per cent U3 0 8  by weight. No delivery will 
be accepted under this section of less 
than one short ton of refined uranium, 
nor of refined uranium which contains 
by weight less than 97 per cent U3 Oa  in 
black uranium oxide or 87 per cent U3 Os  
in sodium uranate. However, the Com­
mission will be interested in negotiating 
reasonable terms with respect to deliv­
eries of high-grade ores and refined 
products in lesser quantities and grades 
than those specified in this section.
(f) Large quantities or special condi­
tions.  The prices established in para­
graph (b) of this section are minimum 
prices. The Commission may by nego­
tiations establish higher prices for guar­
anteed delivery of lots of ores or me­
chanical concentrates substantially in 
excess of ten short tons, or for lots of 
refined uranium substantially in excess 
of one short ton. The Commission also 
may by negotiation establish higher 
prices for delivery of ores, mechanical 
concentrates, or refined uranium under 
other special conditions, taking into con­
sideration such factors as refining and 
milling costs, transportation costs, and 
other applicable factors.
(g) Other valuable minerals.  In 
making payment for material delivered 
to it in accordance with this section, the 
Commission will give consideration to the 
existence of recoverable gold, silver, ra­
dium, thorium, or any other valuable 
constituent in the light of the cost of 
recovery.
(h) Licenses.  Arrangements will be 
made by the Commission for the issuance 
of licenses, pursuant to the Atomic En­
ergy Act of 1946, covering deliveries of 
source material to the Commission under 
this section. (Sec. 5 (b), 60 Stat. 761)
Effective date.  This circular will be­
come effective at midnight, April 11, 
1948.
Dated at Washington, D. C., this 9th 
day of April 1948.
By order of the Comnjission.
W alter  J . W illia m s, 
Acting G eneral Manager.
[F. R. Doc. 48-3425; Filed, Apr. 19, 1948;
8:50 a. m.]

```

### T8613bee5f733ecb22eb56aff

Text hash 9d5348f7ecc832a1644e38fc9cfc3abe6b46e71f3b7044a33443ae7c131406f5; absolute characters 172–1415; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-9538-authorizing-certification-for-probational-appointment-persons-who.txt

```text
The Civil Service Commission is authorized to certify for probational appointment any person (1) whose name appeared on any list of eligibles at any time between May 1, 1940, and March 16, 1942, with respect to positions generally, or between May 1, 1940, and October 23, 1943, with respect to positions in the field service of the Post Office Department, (2) who entered the armed forces of the United States between such dates and was separated therefrom under honorable conditions and is still qualified to perform the duties of the position, (3) who stood higher on such list of eligibles than another applicant who received a probational appointment therefrom, and (4) who requests restoration to the list of eligibles, or certification, within ninety days after the termination of service in the armed forces or of hospitalization continuing after discharge for a period of not more than one year, or within ninety days after the date of this order, whichever is later: Provided, That this order shall not apply in any case in which the list of eligibles had expired prior to the entry of the applicant into the armed forces and the Commission had not sustained objections to his certification from such list because of his draft status.
```

Administrator and the Secretary of Agri­
culture respectively shall (1) so far as is 
'reasonable, practicable and necessary for 
this purpose, see that such price increases 
do not cause price increases at later levels 
of production or distribution, and (2) 
improve and tighten price controls in 
those fields which are important in rela­
tion to production costs or the cost of 
living in which in their judgment the 
controls have heretofore been insuffi­
ciently effective. ..
rv
1. The National War Labor Board, and 
such other agencies as may be designated 
by the Director of Economic Stabiliza­
tion with the approval of the Director 
of War Mobilization and Reconversion, 
are authorized to provide that employers 
may, through collective bargaining with 
duly certified or recognized representa­
tives of the employees involved or, if 
there is no such representative, by volun­
tary action, make wage or salary in­
creases without the necessity of obtain­
ing approval therefor, upon the condi­
tion that such increases will not be 
used in whole or in part as the basis for 
seeking an increase in price ceilings, 
or for resisting otherwise justifiable re­
ductions in price ceilings, or, in the case 
of products or services being furnished 
under contract with a federal procure­
ment agency, will not increase the costs 
to the United States.
2. In addition to the authority to ap­
prove increases to correct gross inequi­
ties and for other specified purposes, 
conferred by Section 2 of Title n  
of Executive Order 9250, the National 
War Labor Board or other designated 
agency is hereby authorized to approve, 
without regard to the limitations con­
tained in any other orders or directives, 
such increases as may be necessary to 
correct maladjustments or inequities 
which would interfere with the effective 
transition to a peacetime economy; pro­
vided, however, that in /  dispute cases 
this additional authority shall not be 
used to direct increases to be effective as 
of a date prior to the date of this order.
Where the National War Labor 
Board or other designated agency, or the 
Price Administrator, shall have reason to 
believe that a proposed wage or salary 
increase will require a change in the 
price ceiling of the commodity or serv­
ices involved, such proposed increase, if 
approved by the National War Labor 
Board or such other designated agency 
under the authority of this section shall 
become effective only if also approved by 
the Director of Economic Stabilization.
/ 3 . Officials charged with the settle­
ment of labor disputes in accordance 
with the terms of Executive Order 9017 
and Section 7 of the,War Labor Disputes 
Act shall consider that labor disputes 
which would interrupt work contribut­
ing to the production of military supplies 
or interfere with effective transition to 
a peacetime economy are disputes which 
interrupt work contributing to the effec­
tive prosecution of the war.
facilitate the fulfillment of relief and 
other essential export programs; and 
allocate scarce materials or facilities 
necessary for the production of low- 
priced items essential to the continued 
success of the stabilization program.

EXECUTIVE ORDER 9598
M odifying  the C onditions  U pon W hich 
a C lassified S tatus M ay B e G ranted
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
by section 17fÌ3 of the Revised Statutes of 
the United States and by section 2 of the 
Civil Service Act (22 Stat. 403), it is 
hereby ordered as follows:
Hereafter no person shall be accorded 
a classified civil-service status on the ba­
sis of his name having been reached on 
a civil-service register unless he shall 
have been regularly selected for appoint­
ment from a certificate issued by the 
Civil Service Commission for probational 
appointment and shall have entered on 
duty pursuant to selection from such 
certificate, or unless (a) he shall have 
been appointed to a classified (competi­
tive) position for other than temporary- 
job employment lasting for one year or 
less, (b) his appointment shall have been 
prior to March 16, 1942, with respect to 
positions generally or prior to October 
23, 1943, with respect to positions in the 
field service of the Post Office Depart­
ment, and (c) the records of the Civil 
Service Commission clearly show that he 
stood higher on an appropriate civil- 
service list of eligibles than another eligi­
ble who received an original probational 
appointment from such list.
This order supersedes Executive Order 
No. 9506 of December 12,19441 ; but does 
not supersede or modify Executive Order 
No. 9538 of April 13, 1945.*
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse,
August 17,1945.
V
The War Production Board shall move 
as rapidly as feasible without endanger­
ing orderly reconversion and the stabili­
zation of the economy to free business 
from its controls. During the transition 
it shall use all of its authorized powers 
to expand the production of materials 
which are in short supply; limit the 
manufacture of products for which 
materials or facilities are insufficient;- 
control the accumulation of inventories 
so as to avoid speculative hoarding and 
unbalanced distribution which would 
curtail total production; grant priority 
assistance to break bottlenecks which 
would impede the reconversion process;

### T8c85a3ea5f2234499af7bd03

Text hash d8e13515725c91f62673405821165fe9e541bcb53dfadd9cf195e91fe3e61273; absolute characters 199–1659; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-fr-originals-9538-505bf20959.txt

```text
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
by section 1753 of the Revised Statutes 
and by section 2 of the Civil Service Act 
(22 Stat. 403), it is hereby ordered as 
follows:
The Civil Service Commission is au­
thorized to certify for probational ap­
pointment any person (1) whose name 
appeared on any list of eligibles at any 
time between May 1, 1940, and March 
16, 1942, with respect to positions gen­
erally, or between May 1, 1940, and Oc­
tober 23, 1943, with respect to positions 
in the field service of the Post Office De­
partment, (2) who entered the armed 
forces of the United States between such 
dates and was separated therefrom un­
der honorable conditions and is still 
qualified to perform the duties of the 
position, (% ) who stood higher on such 
list of eligibles than another applicant 
who received a probational appointment 
therefrom, and (4) who requests restora­
tion to the list of eligibles, or certifica­
tion, within ninety days after the ter­
mination of service in the armed forces 
or of hospitalization continuing after 
discharge for a period of not more than 
one year, or within ninety days after 
the date of this order, whichever is later: 
Provided, That this order shall not apply 
in any case in which the list of eligibles 
had expired prior to the entry of the ap­
plicant into the armed forces and the 
Commission had not sustained objections 
to his certification from such list because 
of his draft status.
```

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10415
Amendment  of E xecutive  O rder N o.
9949,1  Entitled  “ Modifying  E xecutive  
Order No. 9721, P roviding  for the 
Transfer, of P ersonnel  to C ertain 
Public International  O rganizations ”
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
by the Civil Service Act (22 Stat. 403) 
and section 1753 of the Revised Statutes, 
and as President of the United States, I 
hereby amend Executive Order No. 9949, 
entitled “Modifying Executive Order' No. 
9721, Providing for the Transfer of 
Personnel to Certain Public Interna­
tional Organizations” , by striking out the 
words “within five years from the date 
of his transfer to such Board” , occurring 
in the second paragraph of the said or­
der, and inserting in lieu thereof the 
words “on or before December 31, 1953”.

Washington, Saturday, October 1, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10080

By virtue of the authority vested in me 
by section 2 of the Civil Service Act (22 
Stat. 404) and by section 1753 of the Re­
vised Statutes of the United States, it is 
hereby ordered as follows:
The incumbent in an active duty status 
of any office or position in the competitive 
service of the Federal Government on 
the date of this order who is without 
competitive status shall, upon recom­
mendation made within the period of one 
year from the date of this order by the 
head of the agency in which he is em­
ployed, acquire a competitive civil serv­
ice status if all of the following 
conditions are satisfied: (1) That such 
incumbent was appointed to an office 
or position in the executive branch of the 
Federal Government prior to March 16, 
1942 (the date on which the War Serv­
ice Regulations became effective), and 
has had continuous service with the Fed­
eral Government since that date which 
is creditable for retirement purposes, 
inclusive of any intervening military 
service; ( 2) that if the employment of 
such incumbent is evaluated under an 
efficiency rating system his most recent 
rating is “Good” or better, and if his 
employment is not evaluated under an 
efficiency rating system the head of the 
agency concerned has certified to the 
Civil Service Commission that the in­
cumbent has served with merit for six 
months or longer immediately prior to 
the date of such certification; (3) that 
such person successfully qualifies in such 
suitable noncompetitive examination as 
the Civil Service Commission may pre­
scribe; and (4) that such incumbent 
shall be given only one such noncompeti­
tive examination: Provided, that sepa­
ration for one year or less due to 
reduction of force shall not prevent the 
acquisition of a competitive status here­
under by the present incumbent in an 
active duty status of an office or position 
In the competitive service.
The Civil Service Commission shall 
promulgate regulations to effectuate the 
purposes of this order.
H arry  S. T r u m a n
T h e W h i t e H o u s e
September 30, 1949.
[P. R. Doc. 49-7996; Piled, Sept. 80, 1949;
12:14 p. m.]

The term “preference eligible employees’ *

following persons:
(i) Those ex-service men and women 
who have served on active duty in any

States and have been separated there­
from under honorable conditions and 
who have established the present exist­
ence of a service-connected disability or 
who are receiving compensation, dis­
ability retirement benefits, or pension by 
reason of public laws administered by 
the Veterans’  Administration, the War 
Department or the Navy Department;
(ii) The wives of such service-con­
nected disabled ex-servicemen as have 
themselves been unable to qualify for 
any civil service appointment;
(iii) The unmarried widows of de­
ceased ex-servicemen who served on ac­
tive duty in any branch of the armed 
forces of the United States during any 
war, or in any campaign or expedition 
(for which a campaign badge has been 
authorized), and who were separated 
therefrom under honorable conditions;
(iv) Those ex-servicemen and women 
who have served on active duty in any

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 11303
MODIFYING EXECUTIVE ORDER NO. 9721, AS AMENDED BY EXECUTIVE  
ORDER NO. 10103, PROVIDING FOR THE TRANSFER OF PERSONNEL  
TO CERTAIN PUBLIC INTERNATIONAL ORGANIZATIONS
By virtue of the authority vested in me by the Civil Service Act 
(22 Stat. 403) and Section 1753 of the Revised Statutes, and as Presi­
dent of the United States, it is ordered that Executive Order No. 9721 
of May 10, 1946, as amended by Executive Order No. 10103 of 
February 1, 1950, providing for the transfer of personnel to public 
international organizations in which the United States Government 
participates be, and it is hereby, modified as follows:
The limitation in Section 1 relating to the period during which an 
employee shall be considered as on leave of absence from his employ­
ment with the Federal Government is waived with respect to Reginald 
C. Price, and he shall be considered to have been on leave of absence for 
the period March 9,1954, through September 12,1957, for the purpose 
of all rights and benefits provided by Executive Order No. 9721, as 
amended.
T he  W h it e H ouse,
September 1% , 1966.
L yndon  B. J ohnson
[F.R. Doc. 66-10084 ; Filed, Sept. 12,1966 ; 12: 25 p.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VO L 31, NO. 177— TUESDAY, SEPTEMBER 13, 1966

lin n tA i1  £ U £ IX rlL REGISTER
VOLUME 1 1
1 9 3 4
*  V W lT E O  % NUMBER 110
Washington, Thursday ,  June 6, 1946
The President
EXECUTIVE ORDER *0733
Authorizing  C ertification  for P roba-
ttonal A ppointment  o f C ertain
Classes of P ersons
By virtue of the authority vested in  
me by section 1753 of the Revised Stat­
utes and by section 2 of the Civil Serv­
ice Act (22 Stat. 403), it is hereby ordered 
as follows:
1 . The Civil Service Commission is au­
thorized and directed to certify for pro-  
bational appointment any person (1)  
Whose name appeared on any list of eli-  
gibles at any time between May 1, 1940,  
and March 16,1942, with respect to posi­
tions generally, or between May 1, 1940,  
and October 23,1943, with respect to po­
sitions in the field sérvice of the Post  
Office Department, (2) who entered the  
armed forces of the United States be­
tween such dates and was separated  
therefrom under honorable conditions  
and is still qualified" to perform the du­
ties of the position, (3) who stood higher  
on such list of eligibles than another ap­
plicant who received a probational ap­
pointment therefrom, and (4) who re­
quests restoration to the list of eligibles,  
or certification, within ninety days after  
the termination of service in the armed  
forces or of hospitalization continuing  
after discharge for a , period of not more  
than one year, or within ninety days  
after the date of this order, whichever is  
later: Provided, That this order shall not  
apply in any case in which the list of  
eligibles had expired prior to the entry  
of the applicant into the armed forces  
and the Commission had not sustained  
objections to his certification from such  
list because of his draft status.
2. The Commission is also authorized  
and directed to certify for probational  
appointment, to fill existing vacancies in 
appropriate positions and to replace tem­
porary and war-service appointees who  
do not have eligibility for permanent  
retention, the following classes of per­
sons: (a) eligibles who qualify in exam­
inations under section 10 of the Veter­
ans’ Preference Act of 1944 (58 Stat.  
390) and who are entitled to 10-point  
preference and priority in certification  
under that Act, and (b) eligibles en­
titled to priority in certification for ap­
pointment under paragraph 1 of this  
order.
3. This order supersedes Executive Or­
der No. 9538 of April 13, 1945, entitled  
“Authorizing Certification for Proba­
tional Appointment of Persons Who Lost  
Opportunity for Probational Appoint­
ment because of Entry into the Armed  
Forces.”
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite H ouse,
June 4, 1946.
[F: R. Doc. 46-9465; Filed, June 4, 1946;
4:21 p. m.]

HUDDERSFIELD WORSTED MILLS CORP.
§  3.6 (a) Advertising falsely or mislead­
ingly—Business status, advantages or  
connections of advertiser  —  Producer 
status of dealer or seller—Manufacturer:  
§  3.6 (c) Advertising falsely or mislead­
ingly—Composition of goods:  I 3.6 (cc)  
Advertising falsely or misleadingly — 
Source or origin—Place—Foreign, in-gen- 
eral: §  3.66 (a7) Misbranding or mis­
labeling— Composition:  §  3.66 (g) Mis­
branding or mislabeling  —  Producer
status of dealer or seller: §  3.66 (k ) Mis­
branding or mislabeling— Source or  
origin— Place—Foreign, in general: §  3.96 
(h) Using misleading name— Vendor—  
Producer or laboratory status of dealer  
or seller:  §  3.96 (b) Using misleading  
name— Vendor—Products. In connec­
tion with the offering for sale, sale, and  
distribution of textile fabrics, in com­
merce, (1) using the word “Huddersfield”  
in or as a part of respondent’s trade or  
corporate name or representing in any  
other manner that fabric sold and dis­
tributed by the respondent are produced  
in the Huddersfield area of England;
(2) using the world “Huddersfield” alone
or in conjunction with the word  
“worsted” or in conjunction with any  
other word of similar import or meaning  
to designate or describe any fabric not  
produced in the Huddersfield area of  
England; (3) using the word “worsted”  
or any other word of similar import or  
meaning in or as a part of respondent’s  
trade or corporate name when the fabrics 
sold and distributed by the respondent  
are not composed entirely of wool, ex­
clusive of ornamentation; (4) using the  
word “worsted” or any other word of  
similar import or meaning to designate,  
describe, or refer to any textile fabric  
not composed entirely of wool, exclusive  
of ornamentation; (5) using the word  
“Mills” or any other word of similar im­
port or meaning in or as a part of re­
spondent’s trade or corporate name or  
representing in any other manner that  
respondent is the manufacturer of the  
fabrics sold by it unless and until re­
spondent actually owns and operates,  
or directly and absolutely controls, the  
manufacturing plant wherein said fab­
rics are woven or made; or (6) repre­
senting that respondent’s fabrics are  
produced in England through the use of  
the British Coat of Arms or any simula­
tion thereof in advertising or in any  
other manner; prohibited. (Sec. 5, 38  
Stat. 719 as amended by sec. 3, 52 Stat.  
112; 15 U.S.C., sec. 45b) [Cease and desist 
order, Huddersfield Worsted Mills Cor­
poration, Docket 5009, April 23, 1946]
At a regular session of the Federal  
Trade Commission, held at its office in  
the City of Washington, D. C., on the  
23d day of April A. D. 1946.
This proceeding having been heard by  
the Federal Trade Commission upon-the  
complaint of the Commission and the  
answer of respondent, in which answer  
respondent admits all of the material  
allegations of fact set forth in said com­
plaint and states that it waives all inter­
vening procedure and further hearing as  
to said facts, and the Commission having 
made its finding;? as to the facts and  
conclusion that said respondent has vio­
lated the provisions of the Federal Trade  
Commission Act:
It is ordered,  That the respondent, 
Huddersfield Worsted Mills Corporation,  
its officers, representatives, agents, and  
employees, directly or through any cor­
porate or other device, in connection  
with the offering for sale, sale, and dis­
tribution of textile fabrics, in commerce  
as “commerce” is defined in the Federal  
Trade Commission Act, do forthwith  
cease and desist from:
1. Using the word “Huddersfield” in or  
as a part of respondent’s trade or corpo­
rate name or representing in any other  
manner that fabrics sold and distributed  
by the respondent are produced in the  
Huddersfield area of England.
2. Using the word “Huddersfield” alone  
or in conjunction with the word “wor­
sted” or in conjunction with any other  
word of similar import or meaning to  
designate or describe any fabric not pro­
duced in the Huddersfield area of Eng­
land.
3. Using the word “worsted” or any  
other word of similar import or meaning  
in or as a part of respondent’s trade or  
corporate name when the fabrics sold and 
distributed by the respondent are not
composed entirely of wool, exclusive of  
ornamentation.
4. Using the word “worsted” or any  
other word of similar import or meaning  
to designate, describe, or refer to any  
textile fabric not composed entirely of  
wool, exclusive of ornamentation.
5. Using the word “Mills” or any other  
word of similar import or meaning in or  
as a part of respondent’s trade or corpo­
rate name or representing in any other  
manner that respondent is the manufac­
turer of the fabrics sold by it unless and  
until respondent actually owns and op­
erates, or directly and absolutely con­
trols, the manufacturing plant wherein  
said fabrics are woven or made. •
6. Representing that respondent’s fab­
rics are produced in England through the  
use of the British Coat of Arms or any  
simulation thereof in advertising or in  
any other manner.
It is further ordered, That the respond­
ent shall, within sixty (60) days after  
service upon it of this order, file with the 
Commission a report in writing, setting  
forth in detail the manner and form in  
which it has complied with this order.
By the Commission.
[ seal ] O tis  B. Johnson ,
Secretary.

VOLUME 16 1934 ^  Oa/i tED * NUMBER 171
Washington, Saturday, September I, J 951
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10283
Creating  a B oard of I nquiry  T o R eport 
on  C ertain  L abor D isputes  A ffecting  
the  C opper  and N o n-F errous  M etals  
I ndustry
WHEREAS there exist certain labor 
disputes between Kennecott Copper Cor­
poration, Phelps Dodge Corporation, 
American Smelting and Refining Com­
pany, Anaconda Copper Mining Com­
pany, including International Smelting 
and Refining Company, and other em­
ployers who are similarly engaged in 
mining,- milling, smelting, or refining 
copper or other non-ferrous metals and 
certain of their employees represented 
by certain labor organizations, including 
the International Union of Mine, Mill 
and Smelter Workers, several Railroad 
Brotherhoods, and-unions affiliated with 
the American Federation of Labor; and 
WHEREAS in my opinion such dis­
putes have resulted, or threaten to result, 
in strikes or lockouts affecting a substan­
tial part of the copper and non-ferrous 
metals industry, an industry engaged in 
trade and commerce among the several 
states and with foreign nations, and in 
the production of goods for commerce, 
which strikes or lockouts, if permitted to 
occur or continue, will imperil the na­
tional health and safety;
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 206 of 
the Labor-Management Relations Act, 
1947 (Public Law 101, 80th Congress), I  
hereby create a Board of Inquiry, con­
sisting of such members as I  shall 
appoint, to inquire into the issues in­
volved in such disputes.
The Board shall have powers and 
duties as set forth in Title II of the said 
Act. The Board shall report to the Pres­
ident in accordance with the provisions 
of section 206 of the said Act on or before 
September fourth, 1951.
Upon the submission of its report, the 
Board shall continue in existence to per­
form such other functions as may be 
required under the said Act, until the 
Board is terminated by the President.
H arry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
August 30, 1951.

RESENTED BY THE BROTHERHOOD OF LOCOMOTIVE FIREMEN AND 
ENGINEMEN.

INTRODUCTORY 
The President of the United States, by Executive Order 10314, dated 
December 17, 1951, created an Emergency Board pursuant to section 
10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, to investigate and report 
on certain unadjusted disputes between Pan-American World Air- 
ways, Inc., and certain of its employees represented by the Transport 
Workers Union of 
America, C.

VOLUME 24 1934 ^
*  ^ A //T E O  ^
NUMBER 32
Washington, Saturday, February 14, 1959
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10804
DELEGATING TO THE CIVIL SERVICE  
COMMISSION THE AUTHORITY OF  
THE PRESIDENT TO PRESCRIBE REG­
ULATIONS UNDER THE FEDERAL  
EMPLOYEES INTERNATIONAL OR­
GANIZATION SERVICE ACT
By virtue of the authority vested in  
me by section 301 of title 3 of the United  
States Code, and as President of the  
United States, it is ordered as follows:
S ection  1. There is hereby delegated  
to the United States Civil Service Com­
mission the authority vested in the  
President by section 5 of the Federal  
Employees International Organization  
Service Act (72 Stat. 961).
Sec. 2. Executive Order No. 9721 of May 
10, 1946; Executive Order No, 10103 of  
February 1, 1950; and Executive Order  
No. 10774 of July 25, 1958, insofar as it  
affects any employee covered by section  
6(a) of the International Atomic En­
ergy Agency Participation Act of 1957  
prior to the repeal of that section by the  
Federal Employees International Or­
ganization Service Act, are revoked ex­
cept that each shall be considered to  
remain in effect with respect to any em­
ployee subject thereto serving with an  
international organization on the date  
of enactment of the Federal Employees  
International Organization Service Act  
who does not elect to have coverage,  
under the latter Act pursuant to the pro­
visions of section 6 of that Act, and for  
the purposes of any rights and benefits  
vested under each such order prior to the  
date of the enactment of the Federal Em­
ployees International Organization Serv­
ice Act.
Dwight  D. Eisenhower
T he White  H ouse,
February 12,1959.
IF.R. Doc. 59-1405; Filed, Feb. 12, 1959;
3:37 p.m.]
Title 7— AGRICULTURE
Chapter IX— Agricultural Marketing  
Service (Marketing Agreements and 
Orders), Department of Agriculture  
[Navel Orange Regulation 156, Amdt. 1]
PART 914 — NAVEL ORANGES  
GROWN IN ARIZONA AND DESIG­
NATED PART OF CALIFORNIA
Limitation of Handling
Findings.  1. Pursuant to the mar­
keting agreement, as amended, and  
Order No. 14, as amended (7 CFR Part  
914), regulating the handling of navel  
oranges grown in Arizona and designated  
part of California, effective under the  
applicable provisions of the Agricultural  
Marketing Agreement Act of 1937, as  
amended (7 U.S.C. 601 et seq.; 68 Stat.  
906, 1047), and upon the basis of the  
recommendation and.  information sub­
mitted by the Navel Orange Administra­
tive Committee, established under the  
said amended marketing agreement and  
order, and upon other available informa­
tion, it is hereby found that the limita­
tion of handling of such navel oranges  
as hereinafter provided will tend to effec­
tuate the declared policy of the act.
2. It is hereby further found that it  
is impracticable and contrary to the pub­
lic interest to give preliminary notice, en­
gage in public rule-making procedure,  
and postpone the effective date of this  
amendment until 30 days after publica­
tion hereof in the F ederal R egister (60  
Stat. 237; 5 U.S.C. 1001 et seq.) because  
the time intervening between the date  
when information upon which this  
amendment is based became available'  
and the time when this amendment must  
become effective in order to effectuate  
the declared policy of the act is insuf­
ficient, and this amendment relieves re­
strictions on the handling of navel or­
anges grown in Arizona and designated  
part of California.
Order, as amended.  The provisions in  
paragraph (b) (1) (i) and (ii) of §  914.-  
456 (Navel Orange Regulation 156, 24  
F.R. 939) are hereby amended to read  
as follows:
(i) District 1: 729,960 cartons;
(ii) District 2: 448,140 cartons.
(Continued on p. 1149)

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10138
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves ­
tigate D ispute  B etween  the T oledo, 
Lorain &  Fairport D ock C ompany  and 
Certain of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Toledo, Lorain &  Fairport Dock Com­
pany, a carrier, and certain of its 
employees represented by the Interna­
tional Longshoremen’ s Association, A. F. 
of L., a labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive a section of the country of 
essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Toledo, Lorain &  Fairport 
Dock Company or its employees in the 
conditions out of which the said dispute 
arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
July 3, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 50-5884; Filed, July 3, 1950;
12:46 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10139
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves ­
tigate D ispute  B etween  the T oledo 
Lakefront D ock Company and C ertain 
of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Toledo Lakefront Dock Company, a 
carrier, and certain of its employees rep­
resented by the International Longshore­
men’ s Association, Local 158, A. F. of L., 
a labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the Judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive a section of the country of es­
sential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or any 
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Toledo Lakefront Dock 
Company or its employees in the condi­
tions out of which the said dispute arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
July 3, 1950.

signed May 10, 1939): it reserves 144.30 acres in Benson County, North Dakota, for the use of the Department of Agriculture as the Minnewastena Migratory Waterfowl Refuge, makes it unlawful to hunt, trap, capture or disturb any bird or wild animal within the refuge or to enter it except under rules prescribed by the Secretary of Agriculture, and names the refuge. It has no duration, condition or sunset. I read the complete text of Public Land Order 1704 (23 FR 6110, Federal Register of August 9, 1958). Its title and operative paragraph revoke Executive Orders 8123, 8126, 8129 and 8163, which reserved lands for the Department of Agriculture as migratory waterfowl refuges, and it repeats the tract description of Executive Order 8129 (sec. 12, lot 7 and SW1/4SE1/4, and sec. 13, lot 1 and NW1/4NE1/4, T. 152 N., R. 65 W.). It is signed by the Assistant Secretary of the Interior on August 5, 1958, acting under Executive Order 10355, and has no partial limiter, exception or deferred effective date. That is the first end. The Federal Register disposition note dates the revocation August 4, 1958, but the order's own signature line reads August 5, 1958, so the held text controls. An earlier unnamed instrument, Reorganization Plan No. II of 1939 (section 4(f)), made effective July 1, 1939 by Public Resolution No. 20, transferred the Bureau of Biological Survey and wildlife functions from Agriculture to Interior. It does not name this order, so under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments it is recorded as a later event and a disclosed earlier candidate, not as the end. I read the coverage notes of the three machine searches before selecting them: the U.S. Code search (through 2026-10-09) and the eCFR search (as of 2026-10-14) found no citation of this order, and the disposition-note search found only the Public Land Order 1704 revocation. The Federal Register text layers are machine extracted and were checked only as text; the page images were not inspected.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order has no end date, condition, report deadline or sunset in its own words; the machine own-terms hit is the subject-to-valid-rights phrase, which is not a duration term. Candidates considered: (1) Reorganization Plan No. II of 1939, section 4(f), effective July 1, 1939 by the joint resolution approved June 7, 1939, which transferred Agriculture's wildlife and migratory-bird functions to Interior; it does not name this order and does not revoke the reservation or change its boundary, so it is disclosed as a later event and rejected as an end (the later revocation by Public Land Order 1704 itself recites reservation for the Department of Agriculture, which shows the reservation stayed on the books); (2) the catalog date of August 4, 1958, which the signature line of the public land order contradicts. Searches of the held order-text folders by number found only Public Land Order 1704 and the BLM editorial table of public land orders. No earlier named instrument and no held evidence of abandonment was found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The prohibition on hunting, trapping, disturbing wildlife and entering the refuge, and the naming sentence, apply only within the reservation made by this order and fall with it; the order has no separate standing authority. The Secretary of Agriculture's power to prescribe rules is mentioned in the order, not created by it. No independent command of Executive Order 8129 remains after the revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Public Land Order 1704 has no saving clause. It states that the lands are non-public lands, so no restoration or opening under the public-land laws follows. The valid-rights limit in the order protects pre-existing rights and is not a surviving effect of the order. Reorganization Plan No. II is not a residual of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Public Land Order 1704 revokes the whole of Executive Order 8129: it lists the same tract described in the order, and it has no partial limiter or exception. The inventory is one clause from the first operative words to the sentence naming the refuge; it contains a Federal Register page-break line inside the text, which is page furniture and not an operative word. It also contains the heading-free closing sentence naming the refuge after the page break. The end date is the signature date, August 5, 1958; the public land order has no effective-date wording, and it was filed August 8 and published August 9, 1958, which are later dates and are not used.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"88912f0649c4549a405b7d207517cfbffbb59e84120200e3742b143dd9d656e0","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g01/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g01/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:2","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"88912f0649c4549a405b7d207517cfbffbb59e84120200e3742b143dd9d656e0","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g01/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:2","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8129#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8129#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a","path":"jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/opus-refetch_008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8_03c358ff50.txt","sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a","state":"match"},"sha256":"008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8","text_path":"jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/opus-refetch_008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8_03c358ff50.txt","text_sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"c1ca82e037a2d1404b6be6cc6c38fc88cc064007bfe4164254c5cd11b47d3d0b","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/5437e663677371c8db1016ea81a834f6cca735ff9fa988e08397fb99c73ccd36/576710d4f00ed5b6d8736f3619a041899ab56778d23013637a071b8e7c30674f/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"c1ca82e037a2d1404b6be6cc6c38fc88cc064007bfe4164254c5cd11b47d3d0b","state":"match"},"sha256":"5437e6636773 signed June 12, 1939): it reserves 640.00 acres in Walsh County, North Dakota, for the use of the Department of Agriculture as the Pioneer Lake Migratory Waterfowl Refuge, makes it unlawful to hunt, trap, capture or disturb any bird or wild animal within the refuge or to enter it except under rules prescribed by the Secretary of Agriculture, and names the refuge. It has no duration, condition or sunset. I read the complete text of Public Land Order 1704 (23 FR 6110, Federal Register of August 9, 1958). Its title and operative paragraph revoke Executive Orders 8123, 8126, 8129 and 8163, which reserved lands for the Department of Agriculture as migratory waterfowl refuges, and it repeats the tract description of Executive Order 8163 (sec. 21, T. 155 N., R. 58 W.). It is signed by the Assistant Secretary of the Interior on August 5, 1958, acting under Executive Order 10355, and has no partial limiter, exception or deferred effective date. That is the first end. The Federal Register disposition note dates the revocation August 4, 1958, but the order's own signature line reads August 5, 1958, so the held text controls. An earlier unnamed instrument, Reorganization Plan No. II of 1939 (section 4(f)), made effective July 1, 1939 by Public Resolution No. 20, transferred the Bureau of Biological Survey and wildlife functions from Agriculture to Interior. It does not name this order, so under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments it is recorded as a later event and a disclosed earlier candidate, not as the end. I read the coverage notes of the three machine searches before selecting them: the U.S. Code search (through 2026-10-09) and the eCFR search (as of 2026-10-14) found no citation of this order, and the disposition-note search found only the Public Land Order 1704 revocation. The Federal Register text layers are machine extracted and were checked only as text; the page images were not inspected.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order has no end date, condition, report deadline or sunset in its own words; the machine own-terms hit is the subject-to-valid-rights phrase, which is not a duration term. Candidates considered: (1) Reorganization Plan No. II of 1939, section 4(f), effective July 1, 1939 by the joint resolution approved June 7, 1939, which transferred Agriculture's wildlife and migratory-bird functions to Interior; it does not name this order and does not revoke the reservation or change its boundary, so it is disclosed as a later event and rejected as an end (the later revocation by Public Land Order 1704 itself recites reservation for the Department of Agriculture, which shows the reservation stayed on the books); (2) the catalog date of August 4, 1958, which the signature line of the public land order contradicts. Searches of the held order-text folders by number found only Public Land Order 1704 and the BLM editorial table of public land orders. No earlier named instrument and no held evidence of abandonment was found.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The prohibition on hunting, trapping, disturbing wildlife and entering the refuge, and the naming sentence, apply only within the reservation made by this order and fall with it; the order has no separate standing authority. The Secretary of Agriculture's power to prescribe rules is mentioned in the order, not created by it. No independent command of Executive Order 8163 remains after the revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Public Land Order 1704 has no saving clause. It states that the lands are non-public lands, so no restoration or opening under the public-land laws follows. The valid-rights limit in the order protects pre-existing rights and is not a surviving effect of the order. Reorganization Plan No. II is not a residual of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Public Land Order 1704 revokes the whole of Executive Order 8163: it lists the same tract described in the order, and it has no partial limiter or exception. The inventory is one clause from the first operative words to the sentence naming the refuge; it contains a Federal Register page-break line inside the text, which is page furniture and not an operative word. The end date is the signature date, August 5, 1958; the public land order has no effective-date wording, and it was filed August 8 and published August 9, 1958, which are later dates and are not used.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"aec75bf7a08b80596775ab06f2c4f42dc4e3a297ca52ca5beb63a054d5523262","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g01/packet/supp/reviews/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8163#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8163#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a","path":"jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/opus-refetch_008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8_03c358ff50.txt","sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a","state":"match"},"sha256":"008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8","text_path":"jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/opus-refetch_008e6b7585335a78b228ef1c4657ad75221cad40c82197c7d7be074e75db83e8_03c358ff50.txt","text_sha256":"83989093c48025c295b1b0bf637cd79de147627592dbe86a28b71b285f5f8c3a"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"c1ca82e037a2d1404b6be6cc6c38fc88cc064007bfe4164254c5cd11b47d3d0b","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/5437e663677371c8db1016ea81a834f6cca735ff9fa988e08397fb99c73ccd36/576710d4f00ed5b6d8736f3619a041899ab56778d23013637a071b8e7c30674f/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"c1ca82e037a2d1404b6be6cc6c38fc88cc064007bfe4164254c5cd11b47d3d0b","state":"match"},"sha256":"5437e663677371c8db1016ea81a834f6cca735ff9fa988e08397fb99c73ccd36","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/5437e663677371c8db1016ea81a834f6cca735ff9fa988e08397fb99c73ccd36/576710d4f00ed5b6d8736f3619a041899ab56778d23013637a071b8e7c30674f/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"c1ca82e037a2d1404b6be6cc6c38fc88cc064007bfe4164254c5cd11b47d3d0b"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"90c1db318208d7bff1b Order 9538 of April 13, 1945 authorizes the Civil Service Commission to certify for probational appointment persons who lost an appointment opportunity because of armed-forces service, on request within ninety days after the end of service or hospitalization or after the date of the order, whichever is later. Inventory: one sole paragraph. End: EO 9733, signed June 4, 1946 (11 FR 6127, held), ordered in paragraph 3 \"This order supersedes Executive Order No. 9538 of April 13, 1945\" and re-enacted the same authorization in its paragraph 1 (now 'authorized and directed'). EO 9830 later lists 'No. 9538 of April 13, 1945' for revocation effective 1947-05-01; that is a later formal revocation of an order already superseded, recorded as a later event, not the first end. EO 9598 (1945-08-17) states that it 'does not supersede or modify' EO 9538, so it is a recital and not an end.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the ninety-day wording is an applicant's request period that runs from the later of two dates, including the end of each person's service or hospitalization, so it supplies no fixed end of the authorization and is a deadline, not an end. EO 9598 expressly does not supersede it. No instrument before EO 9733 revokes, supersedes or exhausts it on held evidence. Search coverage: the later-events catalogue (OFR disposition notes from 1937) and the held order texts; Public Land Orders and Civil Service Commission notices printed in issues without an executive order are not searched.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No separate command in the order. After that amendment the order's own dated condition is 1953-12-31: the entitlement applied only if his service ended on or before that date (a condition on a right, not an express sunset of the order). The host order EO 9721 was revoked by EO 10804 Sec. 2 (signed 1959-02-12; 24 FR 1147, held official FR text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10804.txt, offset 1193) \"except that each shall be considered to remain in effect with respect to any employee subject thereto serving with an international organization on the date of enactment of the Federal Employees International Organization Service Act who does not elect to have coverage ... and for the purposes of any rights and benefits vested under each such order prior to the date of the enactment\"; EO 10804 does not name EO 9949. EO 11552 Sec. 4 (August 1970; 35 FR 13569, held) later revoked EO 10804 without naming EO 9721 or EO 9949. Number search: no other held order names EO 9949 (EO_10402 hit is an FR page number; EO_10416.txt is the same FR page as EO 10415; EO_12082 hit is inside a file SHA-256). govinfo PDF text layer, grade original_representation_unverified), headed Executive Order 10103, Amendment of Executive Order No. 9721 of May 10, 1946, Providing for the Transfer of Personnel to Certain Public International Organizations, signed February 1, 1950. Inventory: the enacting paragraph and two numbered amendments (1, adding the three-year leave-of-absence sentence to section 1; 2, reviving and amending section 2 on competitive status, including the sentence about Executive Order 10080), recorded as three clauses; the held page also prints neighbouring orders, which are outside the inventory. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; the table lists both 9721 and 10103 (subject: amending provisions for transfer of personnel to certain public international organizations), and the U.S. Code note at 22 U.S.C. 288 note records that the orders were revoked with certain savings by section 2 of Executive Order 10804 and subsequently revoked by Executive Order 12553. The enacting provision of 12553 has no stated effective date, so it is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25. Earlier partial revocation: section 2 of Executive Order 10804 (signed 1959-02-12, 24 FR 1147) revokes 9721 and 10103 insofar as they affect employees covered by section 6(a) of the International Atomic Energy Agency Participation Act of 1957, except that each remains in effect for an employee serving with an international organization on the date of enactment of the Federal Employees International Organization Service Act who does not elect coverage under that Act and for rights and benefits vested before that date. Because the revocation is qualified by those exceptions, it does not extinguish the commands for the saved class and is recorded as a later event, not as the first end. Later waivers for named individuals (Executive Order 11138 of 1964-01-07 and Executive Order 11303 of 1966-09-12) are later events on the leave-of-absence sentence, not ends. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches were selected after reading their coverage notes (complete; no coverage warning; the USC edition runs through 2026-10-09 and the eCFR search to 2026-10-14, which cannot matter for an order whose end is shown in 1986 or earlier).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) Section 2 of Executive Order 10804 (1959-02-12): an express revocation, but only insofar as the orders affect employees covered by section 6(a) of the International Atomic Energy Agency Participation Act of 1957, and expressly not for non-electing employees serving on the date of enactment of the Federal Employees International Organization Service Act or for vested rights; the saved class and vested rights keep the commands in force, so the revocation is partial and is recorded as a later event with its exceptions. Evidence decides: no source shows the commands extinguished for every person before 1986. (2) Executive Orders 11138 and 11303 waive time limits for named individuals; they amend how the order applies and do not end it. (3) The statute Federal Employees International Organization Service Act (72 Stat. 961) is not held, so no statutory replacement is claimed as an end. EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes the orders without exception.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: clause 1 (leave of absence for up to three years while employed by the international organization, if reemployed under section 3) and clause 2 (competitive status for certain transferred war-service employees, and the sentence treating certain employees as in active-duty status for Executive Order 10080) are separate amendments of Executive Order 9721 but each is an amendment of the same parent order, shares the parent's end, and none has its own sunset shown in held evidence. Under the strike-and-insert ruling of 2026-10-21 each continues, amended, until the parent ends.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: accrued leave-of-absence status, reemployment rights and competitive status survive as accrued effects (state transferred_or_accrued); Executive Order 10804 saves vested rights; the Executive Order 12553 enacting provision has no exception.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: the dossier leads target provision not located: EO:10080, EO:11138 and EO:11303 are resolved. Executive Order 10080's held text does not mention 10103 or 9721 (10103 mentions 10080, not the reverse), so 10080 acts on nothing here. Executive Orders 11138 and 11303 each modify Executive Order 9721 as amended by 10103 by waiving a time limit for one named person; the leave-of-absence limit in section 1 is the sentence added by clause 1 of this order, so they are recorded as later events on clause 1, and neither revokes anything. The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the Toledo, Lorain & Fairport Dock Company and certain of its employees represented by the International Longshoremen's Association, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10138 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order has no amendment in the held corpus. neither order's text refers to the other, so no change provision is The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the Toledo Lakefront Dock Company and certain of its employees represented by the International Longshoremen's Association, Local 158, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10139 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order has no amendment in the held corpus. The dossier lead that each of EO 10138 and EO 10139 is cited by the other arises because both orders are printed on the same Federal Register page (15 FR 4333) and the held text file contains both; neither order's text refers to the other, so no change provision is missing.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:10","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:10","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10139#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10139#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0ac5dcdd605720fec6ccc662136b54b82ffb61e2834c9bd4daecae248d33bf38","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10139.txt","sha256":"0ac5dcdd605720fec6ccc662136b54b82ffb61e2834c9bd4daecae248d33bf38","state":"match"},"sha256":"a55ca3dd83cad5db6e88c0fec3b07fc70ab8f8e415f7088acc756540769c37b0","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10139.txt","text_sha256":"0ac5dcdd605720fec6ccc662136b54b82ffb61e2834c9bd4daecae248d33bf38"}]}]}

and\n(f) Administer an effective labor-management relations policy to support \nthe activities and programs under this order with the cooperation of other \nFederal agencies, including the National Labor Relations Board and the \nFederal Mediation and Conciliation Service."},{"char_end":27548,"char_start":27285,"text":"(d) The Secretary of Defense shall report to Congress on a strategic plan  \nfor developing a cost-effective, comprehensive information system capable  \nof identifying on a timely, ongoing basis vulnerability in critical components  \nand critical technology items."},{"char_end":34922,"char_start":34625,"text":"A u thority,  All previously issued orders, regulations, rulings, certifi­\ncates, directives, and other actions relating to any function affected by this  \norder shall remain in effect except as they are inconsistent with this order  \nor are subsequently amended or revoked under proper authority."}],"exceptions_text":[],"filing_date":null,"id":"ACT:EO:10222>EO:12919@480e0c3648354314","names_target":true,"notes":"Machine lead only; govinfo PDF text layer, grade original_representation_unverified), headed Executive Order 10222, Providing for Certain Transfers to the Federal Civil Defense Administration, signed March 8, 1951. Inventory: three numbered paragraphs, confirmed as three clauses: 1, transferring the civil-defense part of the health services and special weapons defense functions of the Health Resources Office of the National Security Resources Board to the Federal Civil Defense Administration, to remain under the Administrator's jurisdiction until further order of the President; 2, transferring related property, records and personnel; 3, effective as of March 4, 1951. Ending: section 904(a)(2) of Executive Order 12919 of June 3, 1994 (59 FR 29525, held government printing) provides that Executive Order No. 10222 of March 8, 1951 (16 FR 2247) is superseded or revoked; the U.S. Code note at 42 U.S.C. 5195 note says the same and gives the date June 3, 1994. Section 904 has no stated effective date, so the end is read as effective on signing, 1994-06-03. Unnamed earlier candidates, disclosed under the unnamed-instruments ruling of 2026-10-21 (they do not name Executive Order 10222 and are not ends): Reorganization Plan No. 1 of 1958 (the editorial note in the held OFR 1989 Codification says it terminated the Federal Civil Defense Administration effective 1958-07-01 and consolidated its functions into the Office of Defense and Civilian Mobilization) and Executive Order 10952 of 1961-07-20 (the same note says civil defense functions were later transferred to the Secretary of Defense). The same 1989 Codification still prints Executive Order 10222 as in force on 1989-01-20, after both events. The plan's own text and Executive Order 10952 do not appear in the held corpus as acting on this order. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches were selected after reading their coverage notes (complete; no coverage warning; the USC edition runs through 2026-10-09 and the eCFR search to 2026-10-14, which cannot matter for an order whose end is shown in 1986 or earlier).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) Reorganization Plan No. 1 of 1958, which terminated the Federal Civil Defense Administration, and Executive Order 10952 of 1961, which moved civil defense functions to the Secretary of Defense: neither names Executive Order 10222 in any held text, and no held source shows the order's commands extinguished by them; the 1989 Codification still printed the order as in force. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on unnamed instruments, a statute or order that does not name the target does not end it; the end is the instrument that names it. They are disclosed as unnamed earlier candidates. (2) The phrase until further order of the President in clause 1 is an own-terms contingent end; the only held presidential order ending it is Executive Order 12919. (3) The one-time transfers in clause 2 were presumably carried out in 1951 but no held source dates their performance, so no exhaustion date is asserted and the express supersession is the first held end. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: clause 1 (jurisdiction of the transferred functions under the Administrator until further order of the President) and clause 2 (transfer of property, records and personnel) are distinct commands of the same order; clause 3 fixes the effective date. None is shown in held evidence to have a separate source, statute or earlier sunset; each shares the express supersession by Executive Order 12919, with the executed transfers saved as accrued effects.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: accrued effects of executed transfers (state transferred_or_accrued) with the Executive Order 12919 section 903 saving language as source.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: section 904(a)(2) of Executive Order 12919 names Executive Order No. 10222 of March 8, 1951 (16 FR 2247) without exception, so it reaches all three clauses. The dossier note that the 1989 current-law lead lacks an original-source sha256 is resolved by not using that editorial snapshot as a predicate: this is a NO order, and the lead is cited only as an editorial observation that the order was printed as in force on 1989-01-20 before its 1994 supersession.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:16","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:16","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"rejected","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10222#1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10222#1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10222#2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10222#2"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10222#3"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10222#3"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4136b7810d42ba86087ec48ac0b2edda3f8b313cce625fbab92b62ddbf5c16e6","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12919.txt","sha256":"4136b7810d42ba86087ec48ac0b2edda3f8b313cce625fbab92b62ddbf5c16e6","state":"match"},"sha256":"1035fc173d4e4d71bc4e2e140fa037e8c59be84185b6eba09b7f522198d79bbc","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12919.txt","text_sha256":"4136b7810d42ba86087ec48ac0b2edda3f8b313cce625fbab92b62ddbf5c16e6"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"5e744fa498f9cde39f8a2e9b0a14f3e330d8dceee4642fa008d63bf3a5ee9aad","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10222.txt","sha256":"5e744fa498f9cde39f8a2e9b0a14f3e330d8dceee4642fa00
